[[sep-vplan-area-6]]
==== 6. eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

[[sep_efuse_image_test]]
===== 6.1 `sep_efuse_image_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_image_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the write-one-to-set programming path into one-time-programmable memory: bits that are burned read back as one directly from the array before any re-sense, and after a cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the bits that were burned.

*Test Coverage*: OTP write-one-to-set program, array readback, and post-resense shadow.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage an image, release reset, and AXI-read `FEAT_CTRL` while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still low.
. Wait for sense-done; AXI-read `FEAT_CTRL` equals the image golden and is not 0.
. Burn a set of bits through the write-one-to-set path.
. Read the burned bits directly out of the array before re-sensing.
. Program a second bit into an already-programmed word.
. Cold reset, re-sense, and compare the shadow against image-plus-burned-bits.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-SENSE-FAIL-CLOSED
|AXI `FEAT_CTRL` is 0 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still low. The read must complete before sense-done, or the closed side was not seen.
|CHK-PRE-SENSE-OPEN
|After sense-done the same `FEAT_CTRL` equals the image golden and is not 0, so the guard actually opened.
|CHK-OTP-DIRECT
|The first and last burned bits read back as one directly from the array, before any re-sense, and their whole words match the golden. The remaining burned bits are covered only after the re-sense, by CHK-W1S-PERSIST.
|CHK-W1S-PERSIST
|After cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the burned bits — all twelve, the ten random ones and the two from step 5.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The comparison covered the whole image, not a sample.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK-NONVAC is the scope of the compares, not a record of its own: `sep_efuse_shadow_check_seq` walks every word of every field `SepEfuseImage.check_fields()` returns, and the shared scoreboard asserts a non-zero value-comparison count, so a sampled or empty compare fails.

*Model honesty*: the OTP array behind the fuse-command interface is a
simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro. Two properties this entry
reads as proven are properties of that model rather than of hardware:
write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction in the generated model, so
the "bank that overwrites instead of ORs" failure mode cannot be
represented; and persistence across cold reset comes from the
model having no reset on its storage. What the checkers do prove against real
logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming
path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset and the shadow tracks
it.

*Not claimed*: the `final_sec_disable` half of
`fuse_sense_done_i || final_sec_disable` — exposing the shadow before
sense-done — is not exercised here. Only the `fuse_sense_done_i` half of that
guard is proven.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test]]
===== 6.2 `sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Proves that the eFuse AXI-Lite mux serves the CPU and the Key Manager at the same time: the chiplet identifier reads its known value on every CPU iteration, the Key Manager's ownership tags stay intact, and its register payload keeps advancing.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and Key Manager contend on the eFuse AXI-Lite mux.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a fuse image and wait for sense-done.
. Release the Key Manager from software reset.
. Handshake over the mailbox — receive the ready token, send the go token.
. Loop exactly 512 times: read the chiplet identifier, sample the Key Manager's register payload and ownership tags, publish a counter to cold scratch.
. Stop on the done token.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HANDSHAKE
|Both processors are alive and synchronised over the mailbox.
|CHK-UID-STABLE
|The chiplet identifier reads the same known value on every iteration; no bad reads.
|CHK-TAG-INTACT
|The Key Manager's ownership tags keep their expected values throughout.
|CHK-PROGRESS
|The Key Manager's register payload advances and never moves backwards, so the contention window was real rather than an idle loop.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: one aggregate record, `CHK-COEXIST` (`cocotb/tests/km/sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test.py`), covers CHK-HANDSHAKE (the READY marker), CHK-PROGRESS (payload changes under contention), CHK-UID-STABLE (`bad_uid == 0`) and CHK-TAG-INTACT (`bad_tag == 0`).

*Anti-vacuity*: this entry is double-gated and the two gates are independent. The
Key Manager firmware counts its own errors and converts them into the pass/fail
magic the boot scoreboard asserts on; separately the host reads the six values
the firmware published to cold scratch and asserts each one in its own statement,
so a fake pass would have to survive both verdicts. Progress is held to a floor
rather than merely non-zero: the host requires the Key Manager's payload to have
advanced at least 256 times across the 512 host iterations, so a multiplexer that
starved it to a handful of writes fails.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test]]
===== 6.3 `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks the device through four lifecycle states — one preloaded, three programmed for real into one-time-programmable memory — and proves each reaches the lifecycle controller and produces the expected feature-control decode.

*Test Coverage*: Four lifecycle states via OTP program and resense; feature-control decode; `dft_secure` Case 3 vs the TEST_EN strap.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start in the initial test state; check the lifecycle decode against the golden model.
. Program the production state through the ungated write-one-to-set bit, handling injected physical failures by clearing status and retrying; cold reset and re-sense.
. Pin the two disable overrides to distinct non-zero vectors so each decoded feature-control word is a different non-trivial value; check the state reached the controller and that feature control matches the golden model.
. Repeat for the two token-gated return-material-authorisation states.
. After the initial TEST_DEV check with TEST_EN=0, raise the strap, re-sense, and prove `secure_tm_o` follows the strap while `FEAT_CTRL` stays on the same golden. Score `dbg_disable.dft_secure` on both legs against Case 3 (not the strap). The strap is then lowered again; the rest of the walk runs at `secure_tm=0`.
. Inject a broken LC_STATE pair at the LCC decoder input; prove fail-closed FEAT_CTRL=0; release and restore.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LC-INPUT
|The state sourced from fuses reaches the lifecycle controller, for each of the four states — with no forced or deposited value anywhere on the path. The state read back is also checked for legal encoding and a legal forward transition, using the code the DUT returned rather than the code the test programmed.
|CHK-GOLDEN
|The feature-control output matches the golden decode at each of the four states, at the pinned override vectors, with demote at its reset value. The four-state walk runs at `secure_tm=0`; TEST_DEV is additionally re-checked at `secure_tm=1`, and both legs use the same golden because SECURE_TM does not qualify feature control. Demote is not written here — the demoted branches are covered by `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`. See <<lcc-golden-from-spec>>.
|CHK-OTP-RETRY
|An injected physical program failure is observed, cleared and retried to success.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The pinned overrides break all-ones symmetry: the four expected feature-control words are mutually distinct, so a decode that ignored its inputs would differ from the golden at three of the four states.
|CHK-SECURE-TM
|The TEST_EN strap is driven frontdoor. After the initial TEST_DEV sense with the strap at 0, `secure_tm_o` is 0 and `FEAT_CTRL` matches the TEST_DEV golden. After the strap is raised and the image is re-sensed, `secure_tm_o` is 1 and `FEAT_CTRL` still matches that same golden. SECURE_TM does not qualify feature control.
|CHK-DFT-SECURE
|`dbg_disable.dft_secure` matches Case 3 of the DTP ladder (`SIP_DBG & CHIPLET_DBG & SEP_DBG`, inverted) for the same `FEAT_CTRL` each stitch check just verified, at both strap polarities and at every later state of the walk. The TEST_EN strap is not a term: a formula of `!secure_tm` fails when Case 3 and the strap disagree (TEST_DEV with the pinned DIS vectors keeps Case 3 closed at `secure_tm=1`; `RMA_CHIPLET` opens Case 3 at `secure_tm=0`). Dump refusal on the secure chain is a DFT command-class rule, not this SIB.
|CHK-SECRET-BLANK
|KM-secret disconnect, proven as a transition on one image rather than as a single observation. With the strap at 0 each of `CHIPLET_UID`, `SIP_UID`, `SYS_UID` and `CLASS_KEY` is present in the sensed shadow; with it raised the same four fields do not read back their staged values. `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` names those four Key Manager fields and the TEST_EN disconnect of fuse-bank outputs; it does not require a zero readback. Each staged value is asserted non-zero first, so a DUT that never sensed a field cannot satisfy that field's disconnected half.
|CHK-SECURE-TM-PROG-BLOCK
|While `secure_tm` is asserted, a program of LC_STATE bit 96 must complete with `PROGRAM_DONE`+`PROGRAM_ERR` (`PROGRAM_CTRL` bits 25 and 26), not succeed and not starve DONE. That is the software-visible refusal. That the command never reached the OTP bank is not claimed: fail-injection credit re-arms on every `rst_ni`, and the walk must `resense()` to drop the latched strap, so a later retry cannot witness whether the blocked attempt consumed credit.
|CHK-SIGINT
|A force gated by the tb_top `lc_sigint_inject_i` port breaks the LC_STATE pair at the LCC decoder input. Observation is the DUT `lc_sigint_err_o` probe plus an AXI `FEAT_CTRL` readback of 0 (fail-closed, `SEC_DIS` observed 0). Release restores both.
|CHK-SECURE-TM-OFF / CHK-SECURE-TM-ON
|The two legs of the strap walk, taken as a transition on one image: with the TEST_EN strap at 0, `secure_tm_o` is 0; after the strap is raised and the image is re-sensed, `secure_tm_o` is 1. `FEAT_CTRL` equals the same TEST_DEV golden on both legs.
|CHK-SIGINT-RELEASE
|Releasing the force returns `lcc_sigint_err_probe_o` to 0, so the fail-closed state is recoverable and the probe is not stuck high.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

[[lcc-golden-from-spec]]
[NOTE]
====
The lifecycle golden is derived from the specification, not from the RTL.

`cocotb/env/sep_lcc_golden.py` is transcribed from `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` (the
per-LC-state feature-control profile). Under that decode a PROD demotion relaxes its
debug group to `~(SIP_DIS | SYS_DIS)`, Function always takes both vectors, and
`DEMOTE_1` / `DEMOTE_2` act independently on `[23:0]` and `[47:24]`. The import-time
KAT set includes a `DEMOTE_2`-only vector, a both-demoted vector, and a PROD vector
with a `DBG_1` bit disabled.

`sep_efuse_image.py` derives the fuse-field table from the generated map and self-checks
that the result tiles all 256 words. The stitch sequence, image test, address-map probe,
and `fw/drivers/sep_efuse.h` use the same derived table.
====

'''''

[[sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test]]
===== 6.4 `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: The debug-port counterpart to the two-processor coexistence test.

*Test Coverage*: JTAG AXI-Lite and the CPU coexist on eFuse.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; wait for sense-done; publish CPU-ready.
. CPU: seed resetless `RMA_SIP_TOKEN_I[0]` with a distinct nonzero value and read it back before publishing CPU-ready. Then run a free-running loop scoring two reads per pass -- that token word must retain the CPU seed, and the eFuse interface STATUS must keep `efuse_sense_done` set -- while publishing a counter and an error count to cold scratch. The CPU issues no eFuse writes during the concurrent window, so only the read channel is contended.
. Host, concurrently: 16 debug-port register rounds (52 AXI-Lite operations), all expected to succeed.
. Host: attempt a debug-port read of the protected shadow window; expect an error response with data `0xbadcab1e`.
. Host: repeat a register read after the denial; expect success.
. Read the CPU's counter to confirm it advanced during the burst.
. Host: read `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I[0]`, `TOKEN_EOP`, and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH`; expect OKAY. A sample of the token-block allow class in PROD.
. Host: read `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL`; expect an error response with data `0xbadcab1e`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-JTAG-MMR
|All 16 debug-port register rounds complete successfully through the multiplexer.
|CHK-JTAG-DENY
|The protected shadow window is denied with an error response (SLVERR or DECERR) and read data `0xbadcab1e`, not OKAY and not a timeout. `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc` (eFuse lifecycle-based JTAG permissions) specifies the error-slave data and does not name the response code.
|CHK-JTAG-ALLOW
|The MMR window still works after the denial. The shadow map and `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` answer OKAY under TEST_DEV in `sep_efuse_jtag_window_allow_test`, so those two windows are gated per lifecycle state rather than denied on every image.
|CHK-CPU-MMR
|The CPU writes a distinct nonzero seed to resetless `RMA_SIP_TOKEN_I[0]`, reads it back before the concurrent window, and requires that exact value throughout with zero errors. JTAG operates on different token words with different payloads, so a zeroed CPU path or cross-port leakage fails.
|CHK-COEXIST
|The CPU made real progress during the debug-port burst — the overlap was genuine.
|CHK-JTAG-TOKEN-BLOCK
|In PROD, JTAG reads of `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I[0]`, `TOKEN_EOP`, and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` return OKAY. A sample of the `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` token-block allow class, not every `TOKEN_I` word and MATCH register. `CHK-JTAG-MMR` already walks RMA `TOKEN_I`.
|CHK-JTAG-IFACE-DENY
|In PROD, a JTAG read of `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` completes with an error response (SLVERR or DECERR) and the `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc` error-slave data `0xbadcab1e`. Shadow deny is CHK-JTAG-DENY; this is the program/read interface.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: the firmware prints `CHK-SENSE PASS` (real fuse sense completed) and `CHK-CPU-SEED PASS` (the CPU token seed written to the first eFuse MMR word reads back) before it starts the MMR loop, and exits with FAIL if either does not hold. These are preconditions of the coexistence checkers.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_jtag_window_allow_test]]
===== 6.5 `sep_efuse_jtag_window_allow_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_jtag_window_allow_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (JTAG AXI-Lite master)

*Objective*: Prove the JTAG lifecycle gate denies the shadow map and `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` by lifecycle state, not always. `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test` requires a refusal (SLVERR or DECERR) on both windows in PROD; this leaf requires OKAY on the same two windows in TEST_DEV, which is not a restricted state.

*Test Coverage*: JTAG reaches the shadow map and `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` when `LC_STATE` is TEST_DEV.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a TEST_DEV image. TEST_DEV is not PROD and not RMA_SIP, so the JTAG lifecycle gate does not steer those addresses at the error slave.
. Read the shadow map base and `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` on the JTAG AXI-Lite port. Both complete OKAY.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-JTAG-WINDOW-ALLOW
|In TEST_DEV, a JTAG read of the shadow map and a JTAG read of `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` both complete OKAY. The PROD refusal (SLVERR or DECERR) for the same two windows is `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test]]
===== 6.6 `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that the lifecycle security state actually gates the external inbound port — the security-boundary property, not just the register that describes it.

*Test Coverage*: Lifecycle state gates the SMN-inbound port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image with pinned disable overrides.
. Read the feature-control register over the control bus and compare all 64 bits against golden; confirm the debug bit is clear.
. Attempt the same read from the external port; require a decode error.
. Write and read back the demote register over the control bus.
. Re-read feature-control; compare against golden; confirm that the debug bit is set.
. Repeat the external read; require success and the expected value.
. Still with the filter bypassed, write and read back a Scratch SRAM word from the external port, then read and write the first and last word of each unit the connectivity matrix does not connect to the System Interface initiator; require a decode error each time.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROD-FEAT
|In the production state the full feature-control value matches golden and the debug bit is clear.
|CHK-PROD-BLOCK
|The external read is refused with a specific decode error, not a timeout.
|CHK-DEMOTE-INDEP
|`DEMOTE_2` alone opens DBG_2 `[47:24]` and leaves DBG_1 `[23:0]` closed, so the two demote registers act per-group rather than sharing a priority. `sep_debug` is bit 0, inside DBG_1, so it must still read 0 — and the external port must still be blocked, which is a second, independent consequence of the same property. `DEMOTE_2` is driven first: the demote field is write-once-set and cannot be cleared, so this is the only order in which this DUT can show one group opening without the other.
|CHK-DEMOTE
|The demote register accepts a write and reads back.
|CHK-DBG-FEAT
|After demotion the full feature-control value matches the spec-derived golden decode and the debug bit is set. See <<lcc-golden-from-spec>>.
|CHK-DBG-ALLOW
|The external read then succeeds and returns the distinctive value, proving it reached the lifecycle controller.
|CHK-IDENTITY
|An external inbound access tracks the debug bit — refused when clear, served when set. This is a behavioural differential on the external port; the filter's internal bypass signal is not sampled.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both the blocked and allowed cases were actually observed in this run.
|CHK-DBG-REACH
|With the filter bypassed, an external write and read-back of a Scratch SRAM word answer OKAY with the written value, and the control bus reads the same value. This is the live control for CHK-DBG-UNREACHABLE on the same port and in the same state.
|CHK-DBG-UNREACHABLE
|With the filter bypassed, an external read and an external write of the first and last word of the Boot ROM, Reset Ctrl, ICCM, DCCM, PIC, AP remap and STEE remap regions each answer DECERR (no timeout), and the refused write to `SW_RESET_N` leaves it unchanged on the control bus. The filter is not in the path, so the refusal is the fabric's. The SEP AXI4 connectivity figure of `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` gives the System Interface initiator no connection to CPU TCM, Reset Ctrl or System Periph, and the Boot ROM path serves CPU IFI and LSU only; ICCM, DCCM and the PIC are CPU resources. Unit extents come from the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test]]
===== 6.7 `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Program × lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Coverage*: Program then lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk `SPARE0` through `SPARE8` in order. For each spare, program one seed-selected bit while unlocked; OTP readback and resense shadow match the independent image golden.
. Program that spare's own `LOCKS_SPARE` bit, then reject a second bit (`PROGRAM_ERR`). OTP and resense golden keep the rejected bit 0.
. Spare `k+1` is programmed only after spare `k` is locked, so a lock bit wired past its own slot fails the next spare's unlocked-program cell.
. The seed selects only the bit offsets inside each spare. Slot coverage does not depend on reseed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNLOCK-PROGRAM
|An unlocked spare bit programs and reads back in OTP and after resense. Logged once per spare.
|CHK-LOCK-REJECT
|A write-locked spare rejects a later program; OTP and the post-resense shadow match the independent golden (lock bit set, rejected bit 0). Logged once per spare.
|CHK-SPARE-LOCK-SLOTS
|All nine spares (`SPARE0..SPARE8`, lock slots 32-40 in `otp_fuse_controller.adoc`) ran both cells in one invocation. A lock of spare `k` must not block spare `k+1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_program_read_back_select_test]]
===== 6.8 `sep_efuse_program_read_back_select_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense, `+sep_efuse_prog_fail_count=2`

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_program_read_back_select_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip; the OTP bank model drops the first two program writes after reset with no APB error

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects one spare field and two distinct bits in it. Every run walks the same four legs.

*Objective*: `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL.efuse_program_read_back` (`efuse_interface_ctrl.rdl`) triggers a read back after a program. `hw/ip/efuse/doc/interface.adoc` names the two commands `FUSE_COMMAND_PROGRAM` and `FUSE_COMMAND_PROGRAM_READ_BACK` (program with verification), and `program_status` is 0 for no error and 1 for an error. A dropped bank write returns no APB error (`hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/README.md`), so only the verified program can report it.

*Test Coverage*: the program command with and without read-back, against a dropped and a kept bank write.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage an image with every spare field at 0 at `LC_TEST_DEV`, and sense it.
. Program bit A without read-back (dropped write), then bit B with read-back (dropped write).
. Program bit A again without read-back and bit B again with read-back (both writes kept).
. After each program, read the OTP word through `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` and compare it whole against the image golden with the bits that landed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NORB-UNVERIFIED
|Program without read-back, write dropped: `program_done` with `program_status` 0, and the OTP word still holds the bit clear. A controller that always verifies fails here.
|CHK-RB-DETECT
|Program with read-back, write dropped: `program_done` with `program_status` 1, and the OTP word still holds the bit clear. A controller that never verifies fails here.
|CHK-NORB-PROGRAM
|Control. Program bit A again without read-back, write kept: `program_status` 0 and the OTP word gains the bit.
|CHK-RB-PROGRAM
|Control. Program bit B again with read-back, write kept: `program_status` 0 and the OTP word gains the bit.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test]]
===== 6.9 `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that every demote cell produces the `FEAT_CTRL` golden and the `dbg_disable` ladder and gates live external access, and that the demote lock blocks a later demote until `rst_ni`.

*Test Coverage*: Demote matrix vs the feature-control golden, including debug-disable bits.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk TEST_DEV and PROD at DIS=0 through the four demote cells.
. W1S `sep_fuse_dbg` / `smc_fuse_dbg` and check PROD with both demotes set, so those bits close while Case 2 and Case 3 stay open.
. W1S a seed-pinned DIS pair and walk the four demote cells at PROD. Besides the fuse-dbg bits and `sep_debug`, the pair sets one seed-selected bit from DBG_1 `[23:1]` and one from DBG_2 `[47:24]` (`lifecycle_controller.adoc`), in both `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS`.
. Every cell's inbound probe is OKAY iff `sep_debug=1`, else DECERR.
. Every cell's `dbg_disable` vector matches the DTP gating ladder for that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Every cell's `sep_fuse_dft_disable` / `smc_fuse_dft_disable` ports match the compose of that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Lock one seed-selected `DEMOTE` at 0; a later demote write is ignored.
. `rst_ni` releases the lock; the same demote write then lands.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FEAT-CTRL
|`FEAT_CTRL` equals the spec-derived golden for every walked cell.
|CHK-LIVE-GATE
|External access of the LC shadow is OKAY when `sep_debug=1` and DECERR when `sep_debug=0`. Not CSR storage alone.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Thirteen demote cells ran in one invocation (eight DIS=0, one fuse-dbg compose, four pinned DIS).
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK
|With `lock` set and `demote` still 0, a later demote write is ignored and `feat_ctrl` stays at the undemoted golden.
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK-RESET
|After `rst_ni` the lock is 0 and the same demote write lands.
|CHK-DBG-DISABLE
|All eleven `dbg_disable` bits match the DTP gating ladder for the same `FEAT_CTRL` the cell just verified. `dft_secure` is Case 3 and `dfd` is Case 1.
|CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS
|`sep_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 3 AND `sep_fuse_dbg`, inverted. `smc_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 2 AND `smc_fuse_dbg`, inverted. The compose cell keeps Case 2 and Case 3 open while bits 2 and 3 are closed, so a golden that ignores those bits fails there.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test]]
===== 6.10 `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that a wrong RMA token leaves the lifecycle state unchanged and a legal one advances it, and that a comparator collapse or disagreement forces the error code, sets the sticky fault bit and raises PIC source 40 until reset.

*Test Coverage*: RMA token compare, match, mismatch, and lifecycle update.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start in PROD; LC shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden.
. Wrong SIP then CHIPLET token: no match code, program `PROGRAM_ERR`, LC unchanged after resense.
. Legal SIP then CHIPLET token: match code, LC advances, shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden after resense.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a valid token: legal mismatch code, no sticky bit, IRQ low.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a wrong token: legal match code, no sticky bit, IRQ low. `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc` documents this fail-open case; see <<known-limitations>>.
. Collapse instance 0, then two-instance disagree: match status `6'b111111`, SIP sticky bit, `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. Valid retry plus write-0 leave the bit and IRQ asserted.
. A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases the interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LC-FEAT
|LC shadow and LCC `FEAT_CTRL` match independent goldens after each resense.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|A wrong token does not update LC.
|CHK-MATCH
|A legal token updates LC (PROD → `RMA_SIP_1` → `RMA_CHIP_1`).
|CHK-RANDCFG
|SIP and CHIPLET both walked match and mismatch; tokens from the seed.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE
|All three comparators flipping a match to a legal mismatch does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE-MATCH
|All three comparators flipping a mismatch to a legal match does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COLLAPSE
|Inject `3'b001` forces `6'b111111` and the SIP sticky bit. That encoding also makes instance 0 disagree with the others, so this check does not isolate the collapse detector.
|CHK-DISAGREE
|Two instances that disagree force `6'b111111` and keep the sticky bit.
|CHK-ERROR-CODE
|A redundancy fault writes `6'b111111` on every match-status bit.
|CHK-STICKY
|A valid-token retry and a write-0 leave `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and irq39 asserted (`sw=r` until reset).
|CHK-IRQ-39
|`sep_internal_interrupts[39]` (PIC source 40) asserts with the sticky bit.
|CHK-WHICH-TOKEN
|Each collapse sets only its own fault bit. Of the three fields (SIP bit 0, CHIPLET bit 8, SEC_DISABLE bit 16), FAULT holds exactly SIP after the SIP collapse, SIP and CHIPLET after the CHIPLET collapse, and all three after the SEC_DISABLE collapse. A decode in which one collapse sets another token's bit fails at that collapse.
|CHK-JTAG-FAULT
|After `RMA_CHIP_1`, a JTAG read of `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` matches the AXI read. PROD/RMA_SIP JTAG token-block allow is `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`, not this check.
|CHK-FAULT-RESET
|A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. The earlier sticky checks cannot tell a resettable latch from one that never clears. After the two zeros the detector is re-armed once, so the phase ends on a zero-to-one transition.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test]]
===== 6.11 `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip; `SepRmaTokenMatchSeq` drives the frontdoor token compare

*Objective*: The RMA-SIP token digest latch captures a frontdoor hash, holds it while idle, and stops capturing while the DFT test-enable is asserted -- so a scan-visible sticky register cannot be loaded with a real token digest during test.

*Test Coverage*: Digest latch capture, idle hold, and the test-enable freeze on both the digest and its valid bit.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense a `LC_PROD` image carrying the seeded RMA_SIP token.
. Present the correct token over the frontdoor. Watch `digest_latch_en_pre` and `vld_latch_en_pre` -- the pre-gate antecedents -- and require both to activate, then record the sticky digest and its valid bit.
. Hold for two idle cycles: both post-gate latch enables stay low and neither sticky field moves.
. Assert the DFT test-enable through the `token_digest_test_en_inject_i` hook and present a *mutated* token, so a capture would land a different digest than the one recorded.
. Throughout that presentation require the pre-gate antecedents to fire while both post-gate enables stay low and both sticky fields keep their recorded values.
. Release the test-enable.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DIGEST-LATCH
|A frontdoor hash captures into the sticky digest and its valid bit; two idle cycles leave both unchanged; and while the DFT test-enable is asserted the two `prim_and2` gates hold `digest_latch_en` and `vld_latch_en` low, so a second frontdoor operation -- proven to have run by the pre-gate antecedents -- neither latches its own digest nor clears the valid bit.
|===

The freeze leg is falsifiable in both fields. Without the valid gate, `start_i` clears the sticky valid bit at the start of the second operation; without the digest gate, the mutated token latches a digest that differs from the recorded one. The pre-gate antecedents are the positive control: they show the operation genuinely ran, so the frozen outputs are not frozen because nothing happened.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: the test-enable is asserted through a testbench force on `u_sha256_rma_sip_token.test_en_i`, which is DFT stimulus with no frontdoor path and is tied low at the SEP boundary. The force targets the enable only -- never the latch enables or the sticky outputs the checker reads.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test]]
===== 6.12 `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense` so the shadow bank is live

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the eFuse shadow-register write path on every `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE`: set-only rows OR-merge so a bit that is already set cannot be cleared by a write of zero, and SW-writable rows overwrite. Randomization is RANDCFG — every walked row is visited every seed, word index and bit patterns come from the run seed, and the write-policy contract itself is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Every walked fuse-field row follows its specification write policy. The lifecycle nibble stays with the LC W1S leaves.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage an OTP image at `LC_TEST_DEV` from the seeded configuration. Bring up and wait for fuse sense.
. Read every walked row through the shadow-check sequence and compare it to the staged image.
. For each early set-only cell (`SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `CHIPLET_PUBK_REVOKE`, `BL1_VERSION`, `BL2_VERSION`, `REQUIRED_ALGS`), at the seeded word index: read the sensed word (CHK-SENSE). If it carries a 1, write 0 and read again (CHK-CLEAR-REJECT). Write the seeded set bits and read the OR (CHK-SET-STICKS). Write 0 and read the OR again (CHK-OR-MERGE).
. For each SW-writable row: read 0, write the seeded pattern and read it back, then write 0 and read 0 (CHK-WRITABLE-CONTRAST).
. Last, run the same set-only cell on `LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1 and `LOCKS_SPARE`.
. Require every cell to pass, and log the walked counts (CHK-RANDCFG).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SENSE
|The staged value is read back. Staged set-only words carry a known 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words sense 0.
|CHK-CLEAR-REJECT
|A frontdoor write of 0 leaves a sensed 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words start at 0, so this cell is not claimed there; `LOCKS_SPARE` claims it on the unassigned half.
|CHK-SET-STICKS
|A write that sets a bit that reads 0 sticks; readback is the OR. Both `LOCKS` words and the assigned `LOCKS_SPARE` half take this cell from zero.
|CHK-OR-MERGE
|A later write of 0 cannot clear the OR.
|CHK-WRITABLE-CONTRAST
|Every SW-writable row overwrites, then a write of 0 clears, so the OR is field-local.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Forty-one fuse-field rows walked in one invocation: six early set-only cells (`SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `CHIPLET_PUBK_REVOKE`, `BL1_VERSION`, `BL2_VERSION`, `REQUIRED_ALGS`), three late `LOCK` cells (`LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, `LOCKS_SPARE`), and the 34 SW-writable rows. `LOCK` is counted only when both RDL windows are written. `LC_STATE` is not this leaf. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word so the walk does not OR `sep_debug`, `chiplet_dbg` or `sip_debug` into `FEAT_CTRL`. This test drives the CPU-LSU master, which has no inbound filter.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: Scope is the shadow-register write path. Every `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE` is walked. Set-only rows OR-merge. SW-writable `true` rows overwrite, then a write of 0 clears. `LOCK` is both RDL windows: `LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, and `LOCKS_SPARE`. Assigned lock slots stay 0 at sense and are written after every other row so they do not write-lock a field this invocation still writes. `LOCKS_SPARE` stages ones only in the unassigned half; the assigned half is set from zero in that same late cell. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word. Program × lock is a different mechanism.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s]]
===== 6.13 `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_*` (seven leaves)

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_demote_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_chiplet_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_rma_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_transient_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, LCC golden

*Objective*: Prove the next lifecycle state computed on the frontdoor shadow write. This is the second writer of the lifecycle state and the stitch walk does not reach it: that walk programs a one-time-programmable bit and re-senses, which never evaluates this logic. The contract is the specification's three rules — write-one-to-set, a don't-care low bit inside each return-material-authorisation state, and the two hardware-ordered token gates — from which the reachable destinations follow.

*Test Coverage*: Frontdoor `LC_STATE` write-one-to-set, token gates, and the seven leaves: six starting states, with PROD_END walked by two leaves, one per exit.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense the leaf's starting state from a real fuse image; require the sensed lifecycle value to be the one requested, and the security-disable override to be inactive so no feature-control check can pass by being forced to all-ones.
. Present the return-material-authorisation tokens the cell needs, and require each to reach a match code before relying on it — an unmatched token would let a gate check pass for the wrong reason.
. Write the lifecycle word write-one-to-set, and require both the differentially encoded lifecycle word and the controller's decoded feature-control output to match the specification-derived golden for the expected next state.
. Re-check the observed step against the transition rules in value form, using the code the device returned rather than the one the test wrote.
. On the demoted leaf, assert both demotion registers first and prove the demotion is visible in feature control before requiring the transition to succeed.
. On the production leaf, present a valid SIP token with the comparator collapsed; the bit must not set. Release the fault and the same write then advances.
. On the production-end leaf, leave the sensed state by one of two exits, selected by the seed: a write of the ungated bit 0, or the RMA path (an RMA_SIP token match, then a write of bit 1). Either exit must land on INVALID with feature control 0. Then write the lifecycle bits the exit left clear, with both tokens matched; the state must hold.
. On the transient leaf, require the state to hold with the enable armed and no token, then present the token and take no bus write at all; the same rules must apply.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Leaves |Contract
|CHK-START-SENSED
|prod, rma_sip, rma_chiplet, prod_end, prod_end_rma
|Before the first write, a frontdoor read of `LC_STATE` returns the sensed starting state the leaf claims. The bring-up already compares the sensed nibble on the shadow probe; this cell confirms the same value over the bus the walk writes on.
|CHK-W1S-NO-CLEAR
|prod, transient
|A write of zero to the lifecycle word clears nothing. Write-one-to-set means a bit never returns to 0, so a controller that treated the write as an assignment fails here.
|CHK-GATE-SIP
|prod
|The system-in-package bit does not set without a matching token. The write is otherwise identical to the one that succeeds later in the same leaf, so the gate is the only difference between the two outcomes.
|CHK-GATE-SIP-FAULT
|prod
|A valid SIP token presented with the comparator collapsed must not set the bit. The status is the fault code `6'b111111`, which a gate written as "not a mismatch" would accept. The cell that follows presents the same token with the fault removed and does advance.
|CHK-GATE-CHIP-ORD
|prod
|The chiplet bit does not set from the production state even with the chiplet token *already matched*, because the system-in-package state is not yet established. This isolates the ordering requirement from the token requirement — with the token gate open, only the ordering gate can hold.
|CHK-GATE-CHIP-TOK
|rma_sip
|The complementary half: from the system-in-package state, the chiplet bit does not set without a chiplet token match.
|CHK-SIP-ADVANCE
|prod
|With a matching token the production part advances to the system-in-package state, and feature control decodes the new state. Together with CHK-GATE-SIP this is an allow and a block observed under one image.
|CHK-DEMOTE-OPEN
|prod_demote
|Both demotion registers read back asserted AND the decoded feature-control word matches the golden *with demotion applied*. This is what stops CHK-DEMOTE-NO-BLOCK from passing vacuously: a leaf that silently failed to demote would fail this check first.
|CHK-DEMOTE-NO-BLOCK
|prod_demote
|The same authorized advance succeeds while both demotion groups are asserted. Demotion is volatile debug state and not a lifecycle term, so a demoted part must transition exactly as a non-demoted one does.
|CHK-RMA-BIT0
|rma_sip, rma_chiplet
|The low bit sets inside the system-in-package state and, on its own leaf, inside the chiplet state. The specification writes these encodings with a don't-care low bit, so this is a move within one state rather than a transition out of it.
|CHK-CHIP-ADVANCE
|rma_sip
|With the chiplet token matched and the system-in-package state established, the chiplet bit sets and feature control decodes the chiplet profile.
|CHK-INVALID-REACH
|rma_sip
|A write whose result falls outside the named encodings lands there, reached from a state the same leaf advanced into legitimately. Feature control must then read exactly 0 — the fail-closed default. Under the pinned disable vectors no named state decodes to 0, so this discriminates rather than coinciding.
|CHK-INVALID-TERM
|prod_end, prod_end_rma
|From the INVALID state the exit reached, the lifecycle bits the exit left clear are written with both tokens matched, and `RMA_SIP_TOKEN_MATCH` re-read as the match code after the RMA_CHIPLET match. After the bit-0 exit (`0x9`) those are bits 1 and 2; after the bit-1 exit (`0xA`) they are bits 0 and 2, and bit 0 is ungated. The write changes nothing. An ordinary write-one-to-set implementation would set bits the gates leave open, so the held value proves the end-of-life state is terminal.
|CHK-PROD-END-INVALID
|prod_end, prod_end_rma
|From the terminal product state, both exits land outside the named set — the specification's "the only permitted transition is to INVALID", proven rather than assumed. One sensed start allows one exit, so each exit is its own leaf (`sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test` pins bit 0, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_rma_test` pins the RMA path): a write of the ungated bit 0 lands on `0x9`, or the RMA path — an RMA_SIP token match, then a write of bit 1 — lands on the golden `lc_state_next(PROD_END, 0x2, sip_match=True)` value `0xA`, not on a named RMA state, which the specification forbids from PROD_END. Feature control reads 0 after either exit. Each leaf pins its exit with `+lc_prod_end_exit=bit0\|rma_sip`; without the plusarg the seed selects one.
|CHK-TRANSIENT-NO-TOKEN
|transient
|With the transient enable armed and no token presented, the sensed terminal product state holds. The transient path applies the token gates; it does not move on the enable bit alone.
|CHK-TRANSIENT-INVALID
|transient
|The transient return-material-authorisation path applies the same write-one-to-set and token rules with no bus request at all, including when the result falls outside the named set.
|CHK-UPPER-WRITE
|all seven
|The upper three bytes of the lifecycle word take a plain shadow write and do not disturb the lifecycle nibble. `LC_STATE[31:8]` is the `rsvd` field of `sep_efuse_map.rdl`, so the second of two seed-derived patterns replaces the first. Each pattern carries a bit the other lacks, so a stale value or an OR of the two both fail.
|CHK-LEAF
|all seven
|Per-leaf closing line naming which walk ran, the sensed starting state, and the seed the tokens and upper-byte pattern came from.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every checker named for the leaf under test PASSes; the Leaves column is that per-leaf set. UVM_ERROR == 0.

[NOTE]
====
The expected next state comes from `lc_state_next` in `cocotb/env/sep_lcc_golden.py`,
which is derived from `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` and not transcribed
from the register logic. The chapter gives three rules and no destination table, and
the golden states them the same way. See <<lcc-golden-from-spec>>.

Randomization: the tokens and the upper-byte pattern come from the run seed
(`cocotb/env/sep_lc_transition.py`, which reuses `SepRmaTokenCfg` so this walk and
the token randomized-configuration test cannot drift). Each production-end leaf
pins its PROD_END exit with `+lc_prod_end_exit`; without the plusarg the seed
selects one. Every cell within a leaf is fixed and runs every seed, so each leaf
runs one seed per regression.
====

*Leaves*: `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_demote_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_chiplet_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_rma_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_transient_test` — one module, one sensed starting state per leaf, selected by `+lc_start` and staged at t=0 by `cocotb/dv_sim_prestage.py`.

*FEAT_CTRL liveness across the seven leaves*: the specification makes the feature-control word exactly zero for the PROD_END to INVALID step. On `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test` the first FEAT_CTRL read is the sensed PROD_END profile (`0xF000_0000_0000_0000` with the pinned DIS vectors) and is the in-leaf positive control; the second read, after the write that lands on INVALID, is 0. `prod`, `rma_sip` and `rma_chiplet` also read a non-zero decoded profile at the same RDL-derived address. The deciding check on the PROD_END leaf is still the LC_STATE word itself, an exact compare against a non-zero seed-varying value that cannot pass on a dead bus.

*Why one leaf per starting state*: the one-time-programmable image is deposited at time zero and a fuse holds its state across every reset, so a test cannot re-sense its way to a different starting state; from one image only a write-one-to-set superset is reachable. Each starting state is therefore its own leaf, with the sensed value read off the device and required to match what the leaf asked for.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_override_test]]
===== 6.14 `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`; `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`. The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override resets the crypto engines without isolation, so an engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel; `tb_top` turns off the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold assertions for this leaf.

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed match / mismatch / restore.

*Objective*: A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` says a match does not release the SEP reset, and that the IC_RESET `sep_reset_n` override is an independent step: the override alone releases the reset and leaves life-cycle control closed. `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc` says the match opens feature control without waiting for fuse sensing. `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` records the override. This leaf drives the SEP override inputs and reads the reset probes and `FEAT_CTRL`. The bus access and the IC_RESET TDR write are `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`. A read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word while SEC_DIS is active and sensing is still open must return AXI OKAY. A match while sensing is still open stops the sense, so `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` stays 0. SEC_DIS is for a part whose sense is already broken, and the recovery flow is a reset after the token. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` says the token state is retained across reset, so after that reset SEC_DIS is still active, sense stays stopped, the map stays open and the override still releases the reset. A mismatch token then clears SEC_DIS, and a second reset runs a full sense.

*Test Coverage*: Security-disable match, mismatch, restore, override-before-match, sense-gated reset hold, pre-sense feature control, the same override inputs after the match, the pre-sense shadow-map read, the sense stopped by the match, SEC_DIS kept across `rst_ni`, and a full sense after SEC_DIS clears.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token (`CHK-MATCH` is the bind proof).
. The comparator hashes the written `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` and compares it to the metal expected digest (`hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`).
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, and before any token, drive `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1`. Both reset probes read 1 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still 0 and `sec_dis` is 0. `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden (invalid lifecycle, feature control closed). Clear both inputs. Both probes read 0.
. Write the all-zero token before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Match must assert, and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` must stay 0 while sense is still open (`ext_boot_seq_done_i` is already 1, so sense is the only held term). `FEAT_CTRL` equals the all-ones override golden.
. Drive the override again. Both reset probes read 1 while sense is still 0 and `sec_dis` is 1. Clear both inputs. Both probes read 0.
. Read the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word while `sec_dis=1` and sense is still 0. The checker requires AXI OKAY.
. Watch 4000 cycles. Sense stays 0 and both reset probes stay 0.
. Pulse `rst_ni` with the token kept. `sec_dis` stays 1 and `FEAT_CTRL` stays all ones, both reset probes stay 0, the `LC_STATE` read returns AXI OKAY, the override releases and re-holds the reset, and sense stays 0 for another 4000 cycles.
. Token `1` mismatches and `sec_dis` drops to 0.
. Pulse `rst_ni` again. Sense finishes in fewer than 4000 cycles, the sensed shadow matches the staged image, and both reset probes read 1.
. Token `1` mismatches.
. On a later match, `sec_dis` asserts and `FEAT_CTRL` follows `feat_ctrl_expected(..., sec_dis=1)`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OVR-ALONE
|Before any match, with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `sec_dis=0`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1` make both reset probes read 1, and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden (invalid lifecycle, closed). Clearing the override makes both probes read 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the override alone releases the reset and leaves life-cycle control closed.
|CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET
|A match with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i=1` leaves `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` at 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: a match does not release the SEP reset.
|CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE
|With that match still held and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, `sec_dis=1` and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the all-ones golden, and both reset probes stay 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`: the feature-control override does not wait for fuse sensing.
|CHK-OVR-AFTER
|With `sec_dis=1` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1` make both reset probes read 1. Clearing the override makes both probes read 0 while sense is still 0. The IC_RESET TDR write is `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`.
|CHK-MAP-OPEN
|With `sec_dis=1` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, a read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word returns AXI OKAY.
|CHK-SENSE-STUCK
|After that match, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` stays 0 for 4000 cycles, and both reset probes stay 0.
|CHK-RETAIN-FEAT
|After a `rst_ni` pulse with the token kept, `sec_dis=1` and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the all-ones golden while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the token state is retained across reset.
|CHK-RETAIN-RESET
|In that window, `ext_boot_seq_done_i=1` and both reset probes stay 0.
|CHK-RETAIN-MAP-OPEN
|In that window, a read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word returns AXI OKAY.
|CHK-RETAIN-OVR
|In that window, the override inputs release both reset probes to 1, and clearing them returns both to 0.
|CHK-RETAIN-STUCK
|In that window, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` stays 0 for 4000 cycles, and both reset probes stay 0.
|CHK-DROP
|Token `1` returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x2a`, and `sec_dis` drops to 0.
|CHK-SENSE-CONTROL
|After a second `rst_ni` pulse with `sec_dis=0`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises in fewer than 4000 cycles. This is the live control for both stuck windows. The sensed shadow then matches the staged image, and both reset probes read 1.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|Written token=1 returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x2a`.
|CHK-NO-OVERRIDE
|After the mismatch, `sec_dis` stays 0; `FEAT_CTRL` equals the fail-closed PROD golden.
|CHK-MATCH
|Written all-zero token returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x15`.
|CHK-OVERRIDE
|After the match, sec_dis=1 and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the override golden (all ones).
|CHK-DEACTIVATE
|A later token=1 returns mismatch again.
|CHK-RESTORE
|After that mismatch, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` is fail-closed PROD again.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test]]
===== 6.15 `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`; `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`. The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override resets the crypto engines without isolation, so an engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel; `tb_top` turns off the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold assertions for this leaf.

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — the eFuse image is PROD with fixed `SIP_DIS` / `SYS_DIS`

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` says the IC_RESET `sep_reset_n` override alone releases the SEP reset and leaves life-cycle control closed. `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` says JTAG drives `sep_reset_n` through the SEP slice of the IC_RESET TDR. This leaf shifts `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, sized to `jtag_sep_reset_ctrl_t` and packed the way `jtag_ptap` packs the SEP slice. The TAP instruction that selects the TDR is outside this DUT. The per-port override pins stay 0. `SW_RESET_N` is in the `sep_reset_n` domain (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_reset_ctrl.sv`). No token is written.

*Test Coverage*: TDR reset value, control-then-enable programming, `SW_RESET_N` readback, closed feature control, TDR re-hold, sense completing the release, and `SW_RESET_N` readback after that release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, both reset probes read 0 and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is 0.
. Reset the TDR and select it. Both probes stay 0. The override pins stay 0.
. Capture-DR and shift the reset value back in. The shifted-out word is all ones. Update-DR leaves both probes at 0.
. Shift again with `sep_reset_n` `reset_enable` cleared and `reset_control` held at 1. Both probes read 1 while sensing is still open. The override pins stay 0.
. Read `SW_RESET_N`. The access returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default.
. `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden and `sec_dis` stays 0.
. Shift the all-ones word again. Both probes read 0 while sensing is still open.
. Wait for sense-done. Both probes read 1 with the TDR override off.
. Read `SW_RESET_N` again. The access returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default.
. Read `FEAT_CTRL`. It equals the PROD golden of the pinned image.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TDR-IDLE
|Selecting the TDR at its reset value, with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, leaves both reset probes at 0 on every clock of the release bound CHK-TDR-RELEASE uses. `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` stay 0.
|CHK-TDR-CAPTURE
|The word shifted out of the TDR after reset is all ones: `reset_hold` and every `reset_enable` and `reset_control` are 1.
|CHK-TDR-CTRL
|An Update-DR that rewrites that reset value leaves both probes at 0 on every clock of the same release bound. `reset_control` moves without `reset_enable`.
|CHK-TDR-RELEASE
|Clearing `reset_enable` for `sep_reset_n`, with `reset_control` at 1, makes both probes read 1 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`. The override pins stay 0.
|CHK-REACH
|That read of `SW_RESET_N` returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default, while sensing is still open and both probes are 1.
|CHK-FEAT-CLOSED
|With no token match, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the override alone leaves life-cycle control closed.
|CHK-TDR-REHOLD
|Restoring `reset_enable` makes both probes read 0 while sensing is still open.
|CHK-SENSE-RELEASE
|After sense-done, with the TDR override off, both probes read 1.
|CHK-SENSE-REACH
|After that release, a read of `SW_RESET_N` returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default, with both probes at 1.
|CHK-FEAT-SENSED
|After sense-done, `FEAT_CTRL` equals the PROD golden of the pinned image (`SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` fixed), which differs from the withheld-map golden. It is the positive control for CHK-FEAT-CLOSED: the same register leaves the withheld value once sensing completes.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test]]
===== 6.16 `sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`

*BFM / Model*: none; DUT probes and a one-cycle state force

*Objective*: The eFuse read and program interfaces fail closed. From an illegal state encoding, the read interface withdraws an accepted command rather than carrying it to the fuse bank and retires with an error. The program interface is injected while idle and must recover to a legal encoding without issuing a bank command; request suppression and retirement status are not claimed on that leg. Separately, `read_error_o` is set by a failed or timed-out read and cleared by the next legal request. The word is `cleared by`, not `cleared only by`: the leaf shows the flag is still set once the timeout budget and the enable have been dropped, so the clear is attributable to the request, but it does not enumerate everything that cannot clear it. An interface that resumed from a corrupted state, or that carried a stale error into the next read, could return or mislabel fuse data it never legitimately fetched.

*Test Coverage*: Both FSMs and both illegal encodings: request suppression and error retirement on the in-flight read interface, and legal-state recovery with no bank command on the idle program interface. Plus the read error lifecycle across success, timeout, re-request and guard refusal.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense an `LC_PROD` image and wait for fuse sense to complete.
. Positive controls: a frontdoor OTP read and a frontdoor OTP program must each present a command to the fuse bank, on the same signals the refusal checks grade. Fuse sense does not drive either interface, so it is not a control for them.
. Measure the read interface's in-flight window: start a real read and count the cycles its bank request stays asserted. The injection below is only evidence if a machine left alone would have kept the request high across the same edge.
. For each of `2'b00` and `2'b11`, and for each of the read and program machines: force the state register for one cycle. The read injection lands while a real read is in flight with its bank request asserted, so the request must drop because of the injection. The program injection lands in an idle window and grades only legal-state recovery with no bank command.
. While the injected encoding is held, require the read interface's in-flight command to be withdrawn.
. Release, and on the next edge require the read interface to issue no command, set `error_o` and `done_o`, clear `busy_o`, and not return the sensed control-word value. On the program interface, require a legal recovered encoding and no bank command. The recovered encoding is asserted on both interfaces.
. Repeat the live-read control after all injections, so a machine left wedged by the recovery is caught.
. Read error lifecycle, on the same interface. A completed read reports done with no error. A read given a four-cycle timeout budget -- too short for the SHIM to answer inside -- retires with the error set. A fresh legal request then clears it. Finally, with the `TEST_EN` strap latched and the image re-sensed, a read refused by the guard retires with the error set and zero data.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-READ-ALIVE
|A frontdoor OTP read drives the read machine out of idle, before and after the injections. Fuse sense does not use this machine, so only a real `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` read is a control.
|CHK-PROGRAM-ALIVE
|A frontdoor OTP program presents a command on the program interface, before and after the injections. Every `SPARE7` bit used as program stimulus is already set in the golden image. The interface issues its bank command either way -- that is the only thing these legs grade -- and the array cannot change, so an aborted operation cannot leave the OTP image disagreeing with the golden the end-of-test resense compares against. Both controls are load-bearing: every suppression check below holds on an interface that never issues a command.
|CHK-READ-HELD
|Control for CHK-READ-SUPPRESS. A real read holds its bank request asserted for the whole bank latency, measured on unforced outputs and required to be more than one cycle. Without it, "no command" could hold because the request had fallen on its own across the injection edge, which is true on any RTL.
|CHK-READ-SUPPRESS
|The read interface presents no command to the fuse bank while `read_state_q` holds an illegal encoding. The encoding is injected while a real read is IN FLIGHT with its request asserted, so the request drops as a consequence of the injection: a design that carried an accepted command through a corrupted state fails here. Graded on that interface's own request, because the two interfaces drive separate requests.
|CHK-READ-FAILCLOSED
|One edge after the illegal encoding the read interface issues no bank command, reports done and not busy with the error set, and does not return the sensed control-word value. The recovered state is a legal encoding (`2'b01` idle or `2'b10` wait). No document names the encodings; the legal set is the one the state-inject block in `tb/tb_top.sv` accepts.
|CHK-PROGRAM-FAILCLOSED
|The program interface reports the injected illegal encoding on `*_state_o` and recovers to a legal encoding (`2'b01` idle or `2'b10` wait, the legal set accepted by the state-inject block in `tb/tb_top.sv`), with no bank command issued. *Suppression is not graded on this leg.* The injection lands in an idle window -- a program command cannot be kicked mid-flight without burning a fuse -- and an idle interface has already retired its last command, so `error_o` and `done_o` are both 1 and `busy`/`req` both 0 before the injection. The data term compares the interface's fixed refusal sentinel against an image word, which are never equal. Those four terms are logged and none is asserted as a result. The recovered-state legal-encoding check is asserted. The in-flight read leg (`CHK-READ-FAILCLOSED`) is where the 1/1/0 verdict is earned, and its terms are asserted low before the injection.
|CHK-READ-ERR-CLEAR-SUCCESS
|A read that completes normally reports done, not busy, and no error.
|CHK-READ-ERR-TIMEOUT
|A read that outlives its configured timeout budget retires with done set, busy clear and the error set, rather than hanging or reporting success.
|CHK-READ-ERR-CLEAR-REQUEST
|A fresh legal request clears the error left by the timed-out read. Two controls make the attribution real: the preceding check establishes an error was actually set, and the flag is re-read after the timeout budget and the enable are dropped and required to be STILL set, because either of those writes could otherwise be the thing that cleared it.
|CHK-READ-ERR-BLOCKED
|A read refused by the guard while `secure_tm` is latched retires with the error set and returns zero, never fuse content.
|===

The waits in the error legs observe `read_done_o` on the interface rather than
polling the `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` status field. The legs grade interface outputs, so
the wait has to watch the same signals; a status poll would let a read that
never retired look like one that did.

Both illegal encodings are walked rather than one. `2'b00` and `2'b11` fail the legal-set test for different reasons, and a decoder written as a comparison against a single value would recover from that value only.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: request suppression on the program
interface. Showing it needs the illegal encoding injected while a program is in
flight, and aborting a program the SHIM has already accepted parks the SHIM
write FSM in its APB wait state until the next fuse resense, so every later leg
would resense across a stalled transaction. The difference is in
`efuse_interface_shim` alone: its read path copies the bank address into a
register at acceptance and drives APB from that copy, and its write path instead
tracks the live request for the whole transfer. That is why aborting a read is
harmless. `efuse_interface_shim` is an example shim; its write-path behaviour
is not graded. The idle program injection grades legal-state recovery with no
bank command. It does not grade suppression: the request is already low, so
that term would pass with the RTL's suppression logic deleted.

*Scope limit*: the illegal encoding is reached by forcing the state register, which no frontdoor stimulus can do -- that unreachability is the property the design relies on, so the behaviour asserted for it cannot otherwise be provoked. The force targets the state register only, for one cycle, and never the error, data, busy or request outputs the checkers read; the recovery those checkers observe is the design's own.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_km_public_id_test]]
===== 6.17 `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_otp_id.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image (`km_rom_otp_id.S`)

*Objective*: the public identity fuses reach the Key Manager as sensed. `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` routes `SEP_CHIPLET_ID`, `SEP_SIP_ID` and `SEP_SYS_ID` to the Key Manager, and `km_csr.rdl` exposes each as eight value and eight complement words that `OTP_READ_LOCK` zeroes. The eFuse read lock hides only software and JTAG reads (`otp_fuse_controller.adoc`) and does not blank the hardwired KM port (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, public identity delivery), so both KM views must survive it. The Key Manager block bench drives its own patterns onto the KM OTP input, so only a SEP-level run observes what the SEP delivers.

*Test Coverage*: Three public IDs through fuse sense, the SEP dual-rail encoders and the KM capture, with one eFuse read lock and the KM-side lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw three 256-bit IDs from the seed with no zero, all-ones or shared word, and set the eFuse read lock (`LOCKS[59]`, `[61]` or `[63]`) of one seed-selected field. Stage the image at `LC_PROD` and sense it for real.
. Compare each field on the KM `otp_data_i` input with `{~id, id}` of the staged value.
. Read all eight shadow words of the read-locked field over AXI and require that none returns its staged word. Read the two unlocked fields and let the scoreboard judge them against the image.
. Release the KM. Its image copies the 48 KMCSR words `OTP_SEP_CHIPLET_ID_VAL_0` .. `OTP_SEP_SYS_ID_CPL_7` to KM SRAM, sets the three public-ID bits of `OTP_READ_LOCK`, reads the register back, copies the 48 words again and stores a done marker.
. Compare the first copy with the staged IDs and their complements, the read-locked field included. Require the readback to be exactly the three bits and the second copy to be zero.
. Compare the KM input again.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-ID-PORT
|Each public-ID field on the KM OTP input equals `{~id, id}` of the staged value, after sense and again after `OTP_READ_LOCK` is set.
|CHK-KM-ID-EFUSE-LOCK
|No shadow read of the eFuse-read-locked field returns its staged word, and the unlocked fields' shadow reads return theirs.
|CHK-KM-ID-CSR
|The 48 KMCSR words KM firmware read are the staged ID words (`VAL_k`) and their complements (`CPL_k`) for all three fields, the eFuse-read-locked field included.
|CHK-KM-ID-KM-LOCK
|`OTP_READ_LOCK` reads back exactly its three public-ID bits, and all 48 words then read zero.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: `secure_tm` is 0 and `OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD` is never set, so neither is graded here. The KM image grades nothing itself: it copies raw words, and the host holds every golden.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle]]
==== 6. eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field, Phase 3

Phase 3 entries of this area. Each entry is a seeded random test that draws its
cells of a legal matrix from the run seed, and it walks cells in one invocation only where the walk is cheap. The entries carry the full contract of each test. Each expected value is built from the cited specification or
register description. No expected value comes from RTL, from another test or
from the behaviour of the DUT. Tests that share a stimulus and a run mode are
one test with one leg per matrix. An entry that goes beyond an open test names
that test in its Test Coverage and lists only the cells that it adds. A check
that needs a PROPOSED internal drive is blocked and is marked `Blocked: needs <item>`
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>), and it is not counted as reached. An error inject
or a top-port drive outside the closed set of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` is an added
hook and blocks no check.

===== Rules common to every test of this bucket

* *Random source*. Every random value comes from a seeded, replayable random source, and `--stage sim --seed N` replays a leaf. The stream is not a CSPRNG, so no draw makes a token, a key, a UID or a digest. The bench holds fixed named vectors for the tokens (`TOKEN_T1`, `TOKEN_T2`, `TOKEN_OTHER`, and the all-zero `TOKEN_ZERO`) and for the 256-bit fuse fields, each of eight distinct words that are non-zero and not all ones, with no two fields sharing a vector. A draw picks only the structure: which vector, the order, the shape and the position. No per-seed value is quoted as evidence.
* *Observation paths*. "Frontdoor" is the CPU-side bench master of target `lsu_stub_all_live`. The inbound filter does not act on it, so no disable-vector draw can cut this path. "JTAG master" drives `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_i` and reads `axil_sep_otp_jtag_resp_o`. `FEAT_CTRL` is read as the 64-bit register `SEP_LIFECYCLE_CTRL.FEAT_CTRL` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_lifecycle_ctrl.adoc`, register `FEAT_CTRL`) or at the existing probe `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, because `feat_ctrl_o` is not a port.
* *Approved probes and injects*. An approved item is in the closed set: it is listed in `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` or exists in `tb/tb_top.sv`. Boundary ports: `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `lc_sigint_err_o`, `security_disable_o`, `secure_tm_o`, `secure_tm_req_i`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i`, `lcc_demote_state_1_o`, `lcc_demote_state_2_o`, `sep_fuse_dft_disable_o` and `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`). Read-only probes: the sensed-shadow output `efuse_shadow_probe_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, the demotion lock bits, the PIC source 40 request line (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[39]`), the SEP reset net, the read and program interface outputs (state, error, done, busy and the request valid before the guard) and the RMA_SIP digest latch (`token_digest_*`). Test-side access: the OTP image preload at t=0, the direct OTP word read through the frontdoor read interface (`sep_efuse_direct_read_seq`, which secure test mode blocks and which is not the anchor of a check on the read interface), the JTAG master and the frontdoor. Injects: `lc_sigint_inject_i` (the lifecycle decoder input), `token_cmp_fault_inject_i` with `token_cmp_fault_sel_i` (copy 0 of each channel only), `token_digest_test_en_inject_i` (the RMA_SIP latch only) and the bank-model program-failure plusargs.
* *Observation taps*. An observation tap is a read-only reference to a DUT net. It is not PROPOSED and blocks nothing, and a test that uses one outside `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The observation taps of the tests are the post-guard fuse command tap `efuse_shim_command_req_o`, the OTP bank probe `otp_bank_probe_o` (a read-only view of the 256 bank words, for the snapshot, the bit diff and the per-clock sample), the tap of the 24 token word registers, the digest latch probes of RMA_CHIPLET and SEC_DIS, the comparator rail taps `cmp_match_p_i` and `cmp_match_n_i` (the hook control of the comparator copy select, also sampled by the coverage sampler), the DUT port `jtag_id_i` (the hook control of its drive), and the integrity-error flag probe `jtag_filter_lc_sigint_err_probe_o` at the decoder output of the JTAG lifecycle filter. The coverage sampler adds these observation taps, which no check uses: the SEC_DIS token status `tok_sdis_i`, the sticky comparator fault bits `tok_fault_i`, the hash start pulses `token_go_i`, the SEC_DIS expected digest `sec_disable_expected_i`, the status flags `rd_addr_err_i`, `pg_addr_err_i` and `req_err_i` of `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS`, the unmasked stored shadow `stored_shadow_i`, and the shim response `shim_resp_i` (valid and status) at the `sep` input `efuse_shim_command_resp_i` (`SEP_FCOV.adoc`, eFuse sampled signals).
* *Added hooks*. An added hook is an error inject or a top-port drive outside the closed set of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). It is not PROPOSED and blocks no check. The added hooks of the tests are the top-port drive of `jtag_id_i[31:1]` and these error injects: the comparator copy select at the `token_cmp_fault` injection port, the test-enable inject of the RMA_CHIPLET and SEC_DIS digest latches, the injection `jtag_filter_lc_sigint_inject_i` at the decoder input of the JTAG lifecycle filter, the bank-model sense status inject `efuse_sense_status_inject_i` with `efuse_sense_status_word_i`, and the shim response hold `efuse_shim_resp_hold_i` on the `sep` input `efuse_shim_command_resp_i`. A test adds its error injects to the inject exceptions and its top-port drive to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks>>). Each item is named in the entry that uses it.
* *Hook controls*. Where a hook that does not act could let a check pass, the check has a hook control in the same leaf: the port value at the DUT boundary, a probe that shows where the inject lands, or the effect of the inject in a control step. A leaf whose hook control fails fails with `CHK-HOOK FAIL` and never skips.
* *Blocked*. A check, leg, cell or statement that needs a PROPOSED item is blocked: it is marked `Blocked: needs <item>` and does not run. The leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=<item>` and no `CHK PASS`, and no summary row, log line or coverage statement counts the check as reached.
* *Fuse command observation*. The bank loads once at t=0 and keeps every program over reset, and the bench has no hook that rewrites the bank array. An image rewritten between senses changes only the test golden. A burn shows in a direct OTP word read through the frontdoor, in the sensed shadow after the next cold reset, and in an image snapshot and bit diff at the bank probe `otp_bank_probe_o`. The request valids of the read and program interfaces sit before the guard and cannot show a guard refusal. A check that a refused request changes no bank bit uses a direct read of the word where the read interface is not under test, and the bank probe diff where it is. A statement that no program command reaches the post-guard port, and every statement about a read command at the bank port, read the post-guard command tap `efuse_shim_command_req_o`. The lifecycle output `lc_state_o` has no tap, so no check reads it. The `LC_STATE` shadow word at `efuse_shadow_probe_o` carries `{~raw[3:0], raw[3:0]}` in bits [7:0] (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, section Fuse Fields, row `LC_STATE`) and is the lifecycle state observation.
* *Golden*. Each test builds its golden from the cited specification or register description, and the log prints the source and row of the rule that produced each expected value. A value that the specification leaves open is logged and not graded.
* *Reserved bits*. A graded compare of a frontdoor or JTAG register read covers only the bits of the RDL fields of that register (`efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc`, `efuse_mmr.adoc`, `sep_efuse_map.adoc`, `sep_lifecycle_ctrl.adoc`). A bit whose RDL row is `Reserved` with no access and no reset value has no stated value: the compare masks it, and the PASS line logs it as `rsvd=0x<v>`. A register value in a check or a step of this area is the value of the field bits: `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS` `0x00000001` is the value of its fields, and a match-register value such as `0x15` is the value of `token_match_status` (bits 5:0), in a poll, a read run and a graded compare. A field that the RDL describes with an access and a reset value has a stated value, also when its name says reserved.
